# Code map — every file, what it is, what proves it, when you touch it

Generated by `scripts/code_map.py` from the `Navigation` block at the top of every file
(the standard: [FILE-HEADER-STANDARD.md](FILE-HEADER-STANDARD.md)); CI's `code-map` job
refuses a file without one, a dangling link, or a stale map. Read the
[README](../README.md) first for what the product is and claims; read
[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) for how the layers fit; then use this page to find the
file. `Touch when` is written for a developer onboarding a client repository.

764 files with a header · 1 exempt (listed at the end).

## `deploy` (2 files)

| File | What it is | Tested by | Touch when |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`deploy/entrypoint.sh`](../deploy/entrypoint.sh) | The container entrypoint: one image, the role chosen by the first argument. | untested — no unit test; the container smoke in [`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs the ``migrate upgrade`` and ``migrate current`` roles through the built image | a role is added to the image (a ``case`` arm, [`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md) and the Helm template that runs it); a uvicorn flag changes (keep ``--proxy-headers`` scoped to ``CRB_FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS``). |
| [`deploy/verify-image.sh`](../deploy/verify-image.sh) | The operator's release-verification script: keyless signature, SBOM attestation and (optionally) the tag → digest pin, before an image is run. | [`tests/test_release_verify_image.py`](../tests/test_release_verify_image.py) | never for a new repository; the image repository, issuer or identity pattern changes (change release.yml, the Helm values and this script together — the test enforces it); cosign's CLI changes. |

| [`scripts/audit_runner_commands.py`](../scripts/audit_runner_commands.py) | The runner-command audit: derived checks versus the repository's own CI evidence, for one checkout. | [`tests/test_runner_audit.py`](../tests/test_runner_audit.py) | never for a new repository; a runner or detector learns a new tool — add the tool's evidence pattern and the step names that cover it to ``TOOLS`` so the audit stops calling it a gap. |
| [`scripts/check_branch_protection.py`](../scripts/check_branch_protection.py) | The comparator between branch protection's required checks and ci.yml's jobs (stdlib only; ``gh`` reads the setting). | [`tests/test_check_branch_protection.py`](../tests/test_check_branch_protection.py) | never for a new repository (it compares this repository's own workflow with its own branch setting); ci.yml gains a job shape this scan does not read (an ``include:`` matrix, a job-level ``if:`` that keeps a job off pull requests) — teach ``parse_jobs`` and add the fixture case in the same change. |
| [`scripts/check_commit_subject.py`](../scripts/check_commit_subject.py) | The commit-subject gate (stdlib only; CI's ``commit-subjects`` job). | [`tests/test_check_commit_subject.py`](../tests/test_check_commit_subject.py) | never for a new repository; a Conventional Commits type is added to the convention (update TYPES and [`docs/CONTRIBUTING.md`](../docs/CONTRIBUTING.md) together); a real imperative is refused (add it to the matching allowlist as a whole word, never a suffix, with a test case). |
| [`scripts/check_release_tag.py`](../scripts/check_release_tag.py) | The release tag ↔ package version check and the tag ↔ ``main`` provenance check (stdlib + ``git``; runs in CI's release ``build`` job and in the test suite). | [`tests/test_version_consistency.py`](../tests/test_version_consistency.py) (the version rule), [`tests/test_release_tag.py`](../tests/test_release_tag.py) (the provenance rule, on a temporary git repository) | the tag convention changes (it must change here, in ``release.yml``'s ``on.push.tags`` and in the cosign identity regexp together); the release contract changes what a tag must be reachable from (``--require-on`` in the workflow and RELEASING §2). |
| [`scripts/ci_job_budget.py`](../scripts/ci_job_budget.py) | The job-budget guard (stdlib only), run as the last step of the long CI jobs. | [`tests/test_ci_job_budget.py`](../tests/test_ci_job_budget.py) | never for a new repository (it measures this repository's own CI jobs); a CI job is added whose runtime grows with the product (start its clock and run this with its own ``timeout-minutes``); the threshold policy changes. |
| [`scripts/ci_test_shards.py`](../scripts/ci_test_shards.py) | The shard plugin (pytest hooks, loaded with ``-p ci_test_shards``) and its stdlib CLI: ``verify`` (the partition proof the required ``test`` jobs run), ``weights`` (refresh ``[`scripts/ci_test_weights.json`](../scripts/ci_test_weights.json)`` from shard reports) and ``plan`` (predicted seconds per shard for node ids read from stdin). | [`tests/test_ci_test_shards.py`](../tests/test_ci_test_shards.py) | never for a new repository (it splits this repository's own suite); the shard count changes (the matrix, the job names and ``--shards`` move together — [`tests/test_ci_test_shards.py`](../tests/test_ci_test_shards.py) holds them equal); a shard nears its budget (refresh the weights with ``weights``, or raise N). |

| [`scripts/prevention_from_export.py`](../scripts/prevention_from_export.py) | The offline register builder over an exported ledger (PSV), for the value wave's baseline numbers (ADR-0020's "What the register would do today"). | [`tests/test_prevention_from_export.py`](../tests/test_prevention_from_export.py) | never for a new repository; the export's columns change (``COLUMNS``), or the product's error classes do (``EXPORT_ERRORS`` must map each to the kind the product's rule gives it). |

| [`src/crb/builders/adapter.py`](../src/crb/builders/adapter.py) | The seam between the stdlib orchestrator and the builder registry — rung labels ⇄ ``EscalationLadder``, and ``build_fn_for``, the ``BuildFn`` the run calls. | [`tests/test_builders_adapter.py`](../tests/test_builders_adapter.py), [`tests/test_builders_container.py`](../tests/test_builders_container.py), [`tests/test_run.py`](../tests/test_run.py), [`tests/test_builders_finish_gate.py`](../tests/test_builders_finish_gate.py) | never for a new repository (the sighted test command comes from the runner); a new builder that must run sealed is added to ``SEALABLE_BUILDERS`` in [`src/crb/builders/container.py`](../src/crb/builders/container.py); a new attempt error kind must keep its prefix on the head (the ledger reads it there) and needs a ledger test. |

| [`src/crb/builders/brief.py`](../src/crb/builders/brief.py) | The one brief composer (``compose``), the context-arm name (``context_arm_for``, a seam until stream R's ``crb.core.context_arm`` lands), the leak guard (``novel_tokens``, ``refuse_novel``, ``admit_retrospective``), the replay ``S1`` arm's hook (``S1Arm``) and the rule for which factory ``S2`` rows a reading may count (``counts_as_s2_first_attempt``). | [`tests/test_builders_brief.py`](../tests/test_builders_brief.py), [`tests/test_replay_s1_arm.py`](../tests/test_replay_s1_arm.py) | never for a new repository; a context modifier joins ADR-0026 item 1's grammar (``context_arm_for`` and a provenance label); stream R's context-arm module lands (replace ``context_arm_for``'s body with R's). |
| [`src/crb/builders/budget.py`](../src/crb/builders/budget.py) | The spend instruments every adapter shares — the price table, the ``CostMeter``, the ``BudgetTracker`` — and the ladder-as-data helpers. | [`tests/test_builders_base.py`](../tests/test_builders_base.py), [`tests/test_worker_budget_ladder.py`](../tests/test_worker_budget_ladder.py) | a model is added or re-priced — prefer ``CRB_PRICING_JSON`` ([`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)) over editing ``DEFAULT_PRICING``; a new cap needs a stop reason in [`src/crb/builders/base.py`](../src/crb/builders/base.py) and a place in ``exceeded``'s order. |
| [`src/crb/builders/claude_code.py`](../src/crb/builders/claude_code.py) | The Claude Code adapter — ``ClaudeCodeBuilder`` drives ``claude -p`` as a subprocess and ``StreamStats`` reads its stream-json — plus the login probes ``crb doctor`` and the admin route use. | [`tests/test_builders_claude_code.py`](../tests/test_builders_claude_code.py), [`tests/test_builders_container.py`](../tests/test_builders_container.py) | never for a new repository (``auth``, ``model`` and ``bare`` are per-run or per-worker settings — [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)); a new CLI flag or a changed stream event shape is a change to ``argv``/``StreamStats.feed`` with a canned-stream test; a new model id goes in ``KNOWN_MODELS`` AND the price table in [`src/crb/builders/budget.py`](../src/crb/builders/budget.py). |
| [`src/crb/builders/container.py`](../src/crb/builders/container.py) | The sealed-container path: ``SealedCheckout`` (an export of the parent tree as its own repository), ``ContainerSession`` (the hardened builder container and its egress sidecar) and ``BuilderContainerSettings`` (the worker's posture). | [`tests/test_builders_container.py`](../tests/test_builders_container.py), [`tests/test_builders_container_docker.py`](../tests/test_builders_container_docker.py) | onboarding a repository whose tests need a toolchain cache — add it to ``CRB_BUILDER__*`` ``extra_ro_mounts`` / the builder image, never a mount of ``docker.sock``, ``/`` or ``$HOME`` (refused here); a new host the model endpoint needs goes in ``CRB_BUILDER__ALLOW_HOSTS`` ([`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md)); a new sealable builder is added to ``SEALABLE_BUILDERS`` with an ``overrides_for`` branch. |

| [`src/crb/builders/fixture_gold.py`](../src/crb/builders/fixture_gold.py) | The test-only ``fixture_gold`` builder — an instrument check that replays the commit's own source change so the whole pipeline can be driven with no model. | [`tests/test_builders_fixture_gold.py`](../tests/test_builders_fixture_gold.py) | never for a new repository and never in production — the switch stays unset in deploy/ ([`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md)); a change to what ``gold`` means is a change to the negative control first. |
| [`src/crb/builders/labeller.py`](../src/crb/builders/labeller.py) | The two LLM intent labellers (``OpenAILabeller``, ``ClaudeCodeLabeller``) that implement the core's ``Labeller`` protocol, and ``make_labeller``, which picks one from a run's builder/model/provider triple. | [`tests/test_builders_labeller.py`](../tests/test_builders_labeller.py), [`tests/test_worker_label.py`](../tests/test_worker_label.py), [`tests/test_builders_endpoint.py`](../tests/test_builders_endpoint.py) (the labeller id names the endpoint it calls) | never for a new repository (a label run is started per repo from the UI or ``crb tasks label`` — [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)); a new class in the vocabulary is a change to [`src/crb/core/taxonomy.py`](../src/crb/core/taxonomy.py), not here; a new transport implements ``label()`` with the same total-failure contract and joins ``make_labeller``. |

| [`src/crb/core/miners.py`](../src/crb/core/miners.py) | The miner registry (``Miner``, ``register_miner``), the source a miner reads (``MineSource``, ``source_from_git``), the five miners the product ships (``adrs``, ``owners``, ``lint``, ``tests``, ``change-profile``) and the runner that turns their drafts into library proposals (``run_miners`` → ``MineRun``). | [`tests/test_miners.py`](../tests/test_miners.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_library_mine.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_library_mine.py), [`tests/test_cli_library.py`](../tests/test_cli_library.py) | never for a new repository — a repository's ADRs, owners, configurations and tests are read as they are; a miner is added through ``register_miner`` and the contract above (with a test like [`tests/test_miners.py`](../tests/test_miners.py)'s added miner); a miner's output changes → bump its ``version``; belt 5's detection changes → in crb/core/lint.py, never here ([`tests/test_miners.py`](../tests/test_miners.py)'s belt-5 table must hold); a new source kind a miner may cite is a change to ADR-0026 item 10 first. |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/__init__.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/__init__.py) | The public surface of the oracle-adequacy package — one import point for the scorer, the mutator families, the adequacy gate, the negative controls and the sealed corpus. | [`tests/test_oracle_mutation.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation.py), [`tests/test_oracle_adequacy.py`](../tests/test_oracle_adequacy.py), [`tests/test_oracle_controls.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls.py), [`tests/test_oracle_sealed_corpus.py`](../tests/test_oracle_sealed_corpus.py) | never for a new repository; a new public name in any oracle module is added here and to ``__all__`` together (the server imports only from this package). |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/adequacy.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/adequacy.py) | The oracle-adequacy gate — pure functions from a measured ``oracle_strength`` to a band (``strong``/``adequate``/``weak``/``unscoreable``) and a per-trial routing decision. | [`tests/test_oracle_adequacy.py`](../tests/test_oracle_adequacy.py) | never for a new repository; moving a floor is a change to the routing policy first ([`docs/adr/0003-one-routing-rule.md`](../docs/adr/0003-one-routing-rule.md)) and to [`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md`](../docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md) — then ``ADEQUACY_POLICY_VERSION`` bumps. |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/controls.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/controls.py) | The negative-controls gate — seven deterministic submissions per task through the real replay path, the Python AST transforms, and the report the CI gate reads. | [`tests/test_oracle_controls.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_oracle.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_oracle.py), [`tests/test_oracle_controls_go.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls_go.py), [`tests/test_oracle_controls_js.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls_js.py) | never for a new repository (a repo whose regression control reads ``not_constructible`` needs a wider ``belt_scope`` in its config, not an edit here — [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)); a new language needs its own transform module and a ``_apply_<lang>`` branch; a new control or a changed expectation bumps ``CONTROLS_VERSION`` and needs an ADR. |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/controls_go.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/controls_go.py) | The Go negative-control transforms — pure text functions over the tokens of the shared scanner, plus a small declaration parser (functions, methods, func-typed vars, imports, package vars) they rest on. | [`tests/test_oracle_controls_go.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls_go.py) | never for a new repository; a Go idiom the parser misreads (a new assertion library, generics in a shape ``_parse_func`` rejects) is fixed here with a pure test on a snippet first; ``describe()`` is part of the report's apparatus stamp, so a changed transform is a ``CONTROLS_VERSION`` bump in [`src/crb/core/oracle/controls.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/controls.py). |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/controls_js.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/controls_js.py) | The JavaScript/TypeScript negative-control transforms — pure text functions over the shared scanner's tokens, a small parser for function-shaped units, and the per-runner env-poison planner. | [`tests/test_oracle_controls_js.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls_js.py) | never for a new repository; a test framework whose assertions are not parsed (``_EXPECT_VERBS``/``_ASSERT_VERBS``/``_T_VERBS``) or a runner without a hook is extended here with a pure snippet test first; ``describe()`` is part of the apparatus stamp, so a changed transform is a ``CONTROLS_VERSION`` bump in [`src/crb/core/oracle/controls.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/controls.py). |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/mutant.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/mutant.py) | The leaf module holding the ``Mutant`` record and the ``Mutator`` protocol every mutator family implements. | [`tests/test_oracle_mutation.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation.py), [`tests/test_oracle_mutation_text.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation_text.py) | never for a new repository; adding a field to ``Mutant`` changes the report shape in [`docs/API.md`](../docs/API.md) and every mutator's ``generate``. |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/mutation.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/mutation.py) | The mutation scorer (``score_task``) and the Python AST mutator family, plus the per-cell aggregation and the reports built on their output. | [`tests/test_oracle_mutation.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation.py), [`tests/test_oracle_mutation_text.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation_text.py), [`tests/test_oracle_mutation_v2.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation_v2.py) | never for a new repository (an oracle run needs only a configured runner — [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)); adding an operator changes ``PYTHON_OPERATORS``, the rank table and therefore the operator-set hash — a new ``mutation_version`` (with the apparatus bump it rides) and a note in [`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md`](../docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md); a new language is a mutator in [`src/crb/core/oracle/mutators_text.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/mutators_text.py), not an edit here. |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/mutators_text.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/mutators_text.py) | The token-level mutator family (``TextLineMutator``) for Go, JavaScript/ TypeScript, Java/Kotlin and Rust, with its per-language scanner and profiles. | [`tests/test_oracle_mutation_text.py`](../tests/test_oracle_mutation_text.py) | never for a new repository; a new C-family language is one ``LanguageProfile`` entry in ``PROFILES`` plus a pure-scanner test; a new operator or a changed substitution table changes ``describe()`` — the operator-set hash — so bump ``TEXT_MUTATION_VERSION`` and note it in [`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md`](../docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md). |
| [`src/crb/core/oracle/sealed_corpus.py`](../src/crb/core/oracle/sealed_corpus.py) | The sealed-corpus builder — pure functions from mined ``TaskSpec``s to a stratified, hash-split, sealed pair of manifests and a commitment hash. | [`tests/test_oracle_sealed_corpus.py`](../tests/test_oracle_sealed_corpus.py) | never for a new repository; a new field in a task record changes every ``detail_hash`` and the public manifest — bump ``CORPUS_SCHEMA`` and say so in [`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md`](../docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md) (a re-sealed corpus is a new commitment). |

| [`src/crb/core/reading.py`](../src/crb/core/reading.py) | The reading — registration (``register`` → a ``Reading`` the caller writes as a ``reading.registered`` event), the seeded order, the look rule and its exact operating characteristic, the counting of first observed attempts per distinct change, the hierarchy that finds a cell's standard, and the per-cell budget. | [`tests/test_reading.py`](../tests/test_reading.py), [`tests/test_routing_v2.py`](../tests/test_routing_v2.py), [`tests/test_server_readings.py`](../tests/test_server_readings.py) | never for a new repository; a rule is added (a row in ``RULES`` with its exact P(deliver \| 0.80) — an ADR amending ADR-0026 item 5 first); the counting rule changes (an apparatus bump); the operator fixes another budget (``CRB_READING__CELL_ERROR_BUDGET``); never for a new repository. |
| [`src/crb/core/redact.py`](../src/crb/core/redact.py) | The redactor — ``redact`` and its two capping variants, applied to every string that leaves an execution sandbox before it is stored. | [`tests/test_redact.py`](../tests/test_redact.py), [`tests/test_execution.py`](../tests/test_execution.py), [`tests/test_services.py`](../tests/test_services.py) | a client's CI output carries a credential shape not in the table (add the pattern with a fixture in [`tests/test_redact.py`](../tests/test_redact.py) and a line in [`docs/DATA-RETENTION.md`](../docs/DATA-RETENTION.md)#3-redaction); never loosen a pattern to make output more readable. |
| [`src/crb/core/review.py`](../src/crb/core/review.py) | The review ledger — ``ReviewRecord`` (one human's verdict on one graded row, anchored to the bytes they read), its append-only JSONL ledger, the anchor rule both ledgers apply, and the per-cell review statistics. | [`tests/test_review.py`](../tests/test_review.py), [`tests/test_store_reviews.py`](../tests/test_store_reviews.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_reviews.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_reviews.py) | never for a new repository; adding a verdict or finding kind changes ``SEVERITY`` and every consumer's copy ([`docs/API.md`](../docs/API.md)#reviews-human-verdicts-on-graded-rows, the UI) — bump ``REVIEW_SCHEMA`` if the hashed body changes; the anchor rule is a governance control ([`docs/reviews/human-review-guide.md`](../docs/reviews/human-review-guide.md)) and is never relaxed for convenience. |
| [`src/crb/core/routing.py`](../src/crb/core/routing.py) | The routing rule — ``route``, the one function that turns a measured cell and its reading into ``deliver`` / ``calibrate`` / ``granularize`` / ``human`` / ``do_not_ship`` with a reason, a reason code and every shortfall. | [`tests/test_routing.py`](../tests/test_routing.py), [`tests/test_routing_v2.py`](../tests/test_routing_v2.py), [`tests/test_capability.py`](../tests/test_capability.py), [`tests/test_signoff.py`](../tests/test_signoff.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_capability.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_capability.py), [`tests/test_claims_check.py`](../tests/test_claims_check.py) | never for a new repository (the rule is the same for every cell); changing a threshold, a clause, the rule or a reason code changes what ``deliver`` means — bump ``POLICY_VERSION`` / ``CONTROLS_POLICY_VERSION`` and the apparatus, write an ADR superseding [`docs/adr/0025-routing-v2.md`](../docs/adr/0025-routing-v2.md), regenerate README's routing bar, and update the UI's reason-code copy. |
| [`src/crb/core/run.py`](../src/crb/core/run.py) | The replay orchestrator — ``run`` / ``run_task`` drive one task through prep, build, grade, evidence pack and ledger row, with the builder injected as a callable. | [`tests/test_run.py`](../tests/test_run.py), [`tests/test_builders_adapter.py`](../tests/test_builders_adapter.py), [`tests/test_worker.py`](../tests/test_worker.py), [`tests/test_worker_budget_ladder.py`](../tests/test_worker_budget_ladder.py), [`tests/test_patches.py`](../tests/test_patches.py), [`tests/test_worker_spend.py`](../tests/test_worker_spend.py), [`tests/test_write_pack.py`](../tests/test_write_pack.py), [`tests/test_mine_distinct_change.py`](../tests/test_mine_distinct_change.py) | never for a new repository (mode, ladder and budget are run settings); adding a stage between build and ledger, or a field to the pack or the row, changes the evidence every consumer reads — update [`src/crb/core/evidence.py`](../src/crb/core/evidence.py), the store ([`src/crb/store/ledger.py`](../src/crb/store/ledger.py)) and [`docs/API.md`](../docs/API.md) together. |
| [`src/crb/core/runners/__init__.py`](../src/crb/core/runners/__init__.py) | The runner registry — the closed table from a ``RepoConfig.runner`` name to the class that drives that toolchain — and the package's public re-exports. | [`tests/test_runners_parsers.py`](../tests/test_runners_parsers.py), [`tests/test_runners_setup.py`](../tests/test_runners_setup.py) | never for a new repository (pick a name from ``runner_names()`` in the repo config); adding a language runner means one new entry here plus its module, a runner test module of its own (as [`tests/test_runners_go.py`](../tests/test_runners_go.py) is for Go) and a line in [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md). |
| [`src/crb/core/runners/base.py`](../src/crb/core/runners/base.py) | The runner contract (``TestRunner`` protocol, ``BaseRunner`` plumbing) and the records every language runner returns: ``TestRun``, ``SetupStep``, ``SetupResult``. | [`tests/test_runners_parsers.py`](../tests/test_runners_parsers.py), [`tests/test_runners_setup.py`](../tests/test_runners_setup.py), [`tests/test_services.py`](../tests/test_services.py) | never for a new repository — set ``runner``, ``belt_scope``, ``runner_opts`` and ``lint`` in the repo config instead ([`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)); adding a language means a new subclass registered in [`src/crb/core/runners/__init__.py`](../src/crb/core/runners/__init__.py); changing ``TestRun`` or the fail-closed rule needs an ADR and an apparatus bump ([`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md`](../docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md)). |

| [`src/crb/core/runners/jvm_runner.py`](../src/crb/core/runners/jvm_runner.py) | The JVM runner — ``MavenRunner`` over ``mvn test`` with surefire. | [`tests/test_runners_jvm.py`](../tests/test_runners_jvm.py), [`tests/test_runners_parsers.py`](../tests/test_runners_parsers.py), [`tests/test_runners_setup.py`](../tests/test_runners_setup.py) (the cold-repository and test-phase-plugin cases run setup against an EMPTY private local repository) | a Maven repository needs a JDK, extra flags or profiles — set ``runner_opts`` (``java_home``, ``maven_flags``, ``maven_opts``, ``mvn``, ``writable``, ``offline``; [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md)); Gradle would be a new runner, not an option here. |

| [`src/crb/core/spend.py`](../src/crb/core/spend.py) | The spend rules — the calibrated budget profile and the measured escalation rule, as pure functions over ledger rows, plus the per-run / per-repository policy they are switched by. | [`tests/test_spend.py`](../tests/test_spend.py), [`tests/test_worker_spend.py`](../tests/test_worker_spend.py) | never for a new repository; a cap is added to ``Budget`` (add it to ``CAP_FIELDS`` and ``calibrate``); a bar or a minimum n changes (an apparatus-visible change: the rule is on every row — [`docs/API.md`](../docs/API.md) and [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md) say the numbers). |
| [`src/crb/core/stats.py`](../src/crb/core/stats.py) | The statistics every number in the product is reported with — the Wilson 95% interval, mean, sample standard deviation, a two-proportion z-test and the Student-t critical value the economics intervals use. | [`tests/test_stats.py`](../tests/test_stats.py), [`tests/test_ledger.py`](../tests/test_ledger.py) | never for a new repository; changing the interval method or ``Z_95`` changes every ``ci_low`` and therefore every route — an ADR and an apparatus bump ([`src/crb/core/version.py`](../src/crb/core/version.py)), and [`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md`](../docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md)#3-every-number-carries-its-method must name the new method. |

| [`src/crb/core/test_infra.py`](../src/crb/core/test_infra.py) | Belt 1b's table — which files, per language and runner, are part of the oracle's execution environment (``INFRA_RULES``), and the section-aware comparison for files that are only partly so. | [`tests/test_test_infra.py`](../tests/test_test_infra.py), [`tests/test_grade.py`](../tests/test_grade.py), [`tests/test_oracle_controls.py`](../tests/test_oracle_controls.py), [`tests/test_runner_audit.py`](../tests/test_runner_audit.py) | onboarding a repository whose test runner reads a config file not in the table (a builder editing it would go unnoticed — a belt-1 gap) or whose honest commits keep tripping a rule (narrow it by runner, or make the file section-aware): add the rule WITH its reason and a case in [`tests/test_test_infra.py`](../tests/test_test_infra.py); note it in [`docs/adr/0001-four-belts-and-false-q1-at-write.md`](../docs/adr/0001-four-belts-and-false-q1-at-write.md). |
| [`src/crb/core/value.py`](../src/crb/core/value.py) | The value scorecard — pure functions from ledger rows and review verdicts to the north-star number (working changes per pound, blind), clean → working precision, the process-loss share, the learning curve and prospective routing precision. | [`tests/test_value.py`](../tests/test_value.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_value.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_value.py), [`tests/test_value_baseline_script.py`](../tests/test_value_baseline_script.py), [`tests/test_checks_pooling.py`](../tests/test_checks_pooling.py) | never for a new repository; the register's inputs change — change ``default_register`` only; a new belt that decides "working" joins ``proxy_working`` (and the docstring above); a new failure kind that is a process loss joins ``LOSS_KINDS``. |

| File | What it is | Tested by | Touch when |

| [`src/crb/store/__init__.py`](../src/crb/store/__init__.py) | The ``crb.store`` package — its public surface (engine, session factory, ``init_db``, ``DbLedger``, ``Base``) and the layer's contract in one place. | [`tests/test_store_db.py`](../tests/test_store_db.py), [`tests/test_store_ledger.py`](../tests/test_store_ledger.py), [`tests/test_store_migrate.py`](../tests/test_store_migrate.py) | never for a new repository; only when a new store-level object becomes part of the public surface (add the re-export and the name to ``__all__`` together). |
| [`src/crb/store/db.py`](../src/crb/store/db.py) | The engine / session factory / schema-init module of the store, and the home of the append-only trigger SQL. | [`tests/test_store_db.py`](../tests/test_store_db.py), [`tests/test_store_migrate.py`](../tests/test_store_migrate.py), [`tests/test_store_ledger.py`](../tests/test_store_ledger.py) | never for a new repository (the database is per deployment, not per repo); adding an append-only table means adding it to ``APPEND_ONLY_TABLES`` in models.py AND pinning it in the migration that creates it; changing a pragma or the trigger SQL needs a note in [`docs/adr/0002-append-only-hash-chained-ledger.md`](../docs/adr/0002-append-only-hash-chained-ledger.md). |

| [`src/crb/store/jobs.py`](../src/crb/store/jobs.py) | The job queue — the ``runs`` table driven as a durable, crash-safe work queue. | [`tests/test_store_jobs.py`](../tests/test_store_jobs.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_runs.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_runs.py), [`tests/test_server_routes_learn.py`](../tests/test_server_routes_learn.py) | never for a new repository; adding a run kind means extending ``RUN_KINDS`` here and the executor table in [`src/crb/server/worker.py`](../src/crb/server/worker.py) together (and [`docs/API.md`](../docs/API.md)); changing the reclaim threshold or ``max_reclaims`` default is an operator-visible behaviour — note it in [`docs/OPERATOR.md`](../docs/OPERATOR.md). |

| [`ui/src/screens/Results/MapTable.tsx`](../ui/src/screens/Results/MapTable.tsx) | The design's A7 grid: one row per class, one column per size; each measured cell shows its route as a solid tag, `n=… on … tasks`, the point and the Wilson interval, the apparatus version(s) the rows carry, and one more line — the reason code, or the sign-off state ("signed 15 Sep" / "sign-off due" / "sign-off stale", or "sign-off not loaded" when the sign-offs did not load); "sign-off due" is a link to the form only for a reader who can sign (`canSign`), plain text for everyone else — a viewer is never shown an action they cannot take. The five size tiers are the taxonomy (`SizeTier` in core), not the API's `sizes` (which lists only measured tiers): a tier with no row says "not measured · no attempt sighted" on a pale ground and never a number — the honest state, not a fabricated cell. `licenseSentence` renders "What this licenses you to say" for a signed cell, with every qualifier the claims policy demands. Every element in the grid is a hint trigger: the column headers (`col.map.class`, `col.map.size`), the class row headers, and in each cell the route tag, n, point, interval, apparatus and the sign-off line (`map.cell.*`); the per-cell numbers opt out of the tab order (`tabStop={false}` — a 50-cell grid is not 300 tab stops) while the route tag keeps it, so a keyboard reader still lands on every cell. | [`ui/src/screens/Results/MapTable.test.tsx`](../ui/src/screens/Results/MapTable.test.tsx) | never for a new repository; a cell field is added that a reader needs on the grid. |
